# Draft briefing on Bills sponsored by Malcolm Byrne

**Draft — for review** · Incomplete / qualified research

Original brief: Prepare a briefing on the Bills sponsored by Malcolm Byrne. What progress have they made, and what happened to them?
Reporting date: 2026-11-13

## Bills sponsored by Malcolm Byrne: recorded progress and outcomes

The retrieved sponsorship results identify 20 Bills. Their records show a range of recorded statuses and stages, including entries at Second, Committee and Fifth Stage. Those entries establish the activities recorded, but a status label or a Bill Lapsed Event is not, by itself, a certified final outcome. The available evidence does not establish what ultimately happened to the Bills; the list is not certified as complete, and record freshness is unknown. [a-5de92b960ec14ae3cc05]
_Provisional interpretation — moderate qualitative confidence (provisional)_

The recorded history for Protection of Private Residences (Against Targeted Picketing) Bill 2021 includes Second Stage on 2023-01-26, Committee Stage on 2024-05-23 and Fifth Stage on 2024-07-04. It also records a Bill Lapsed Event on 2024-11-08 and a further Second Stage entry on 2025-05-01. These entries document the stages and event as recorded; the available evidence does not explain their relationship or establish the Bill’s final outcome. Among the more recent dated entries shown in the table, Artificial Intelligence Companion Services (Protection of Children) Bill 2026 and Public Health (Tobacco) (Amendment) (Smokefree Generation) Bill 2026 each have a recorded Second Stage entry in September 2026. [a-4c20958a3d5438a98db2]
_Provisional interpretation — moderate qualitative confidence (provisional)_

Some entries do not align neatly. Seanad Electoral (University Members) (Amendment) Bill 2020 is recorded as ‘Current’, while its history includes a Bill Lapsed Event dated 2025-01-29 and Committee Stage entries dated 2025-01-29 and 2025-03-27. Protection of Private Residences (Against Targeted Picketing) Bill 2021 is also recorded as ‘Current’ alongside a Bill Lapsed Event. The records provide no explanation for these combinations. They are reported as separate entries and do not establish either Bill’s final fate; a ‘Lapsed’ status label likewise does not certify a final outcome. [a-cbf1decb697ec6c71d79]
_Provisional interpretation — moderate qualitative confidence (provisional)_

## Evidence limitations

- These are findings from the available parliamentary records, not a certified complete account.
- The source's last update and completeness are not established. Retrieval time is not source freshness.
- Recorded Bill statuses and dated activities do not by themselves establish a final outcome.
- These are known limits of the research interface and will be made clear in the briefing.

## Unanswered research questions
